Current-main fixed A/B optimization evidence — 2026-10-17
=========================================================

Adoption decisions
------------------

Both predeclared optimization candidates failed the original complete 5% gate.
The production DeviceGuard and CPU dense implementation remain unchanged;
their focused restoration, column-boundary, padding and cancellation tests
are retained. Fresh API, CUDA Session and prepared scopes preserve their
resource contracts. Backend, fusion and execution choices remain explicit.

.. csv-table:: Complete strict outcomes and production decisions
   :class: benchmark-results
   :header-rows: 1

   Campaign,Artifacts,Outcomes,Strict rows,Within 5%,Inconclusive,Regression,Decision
   CUDA device restoration,128,1344,336,128,191,17,Reject production change; retain tests
   CPU dense scheduling,144,1920,480,87,368,25,Reject production change; retain tests

These are per-case fixed-budget verdicts, not counts of statistically significant
speedups. CUDA contains 11 native and 6 unchanged Torch control regressions;
CPU contains 7 native/native-BLAS and 18 NumPy/Torch control regressions.
Matching controls also moved: no causal slowdown is assigned to either change,
and control drift is not subtracted to rescue a failed adoption gate.
No samples, rows or controls were discarded, rerun or replaced after the verdict.
All same-candidate input, true-residual and known iterations/matvecs/restarts
gates passed, including across all four A/B observations.

The common A source is ``445275a3994e342b06de4093889f3f814c8706c6``, published as
``benchmarks/main-complete-20261017``. Actual B sources remain available as:

* CUDA: ``3551afcfee3faa6d4aa718cf9315782cc2ebb001``, tag
  ``benchmarks/cuda-device-guards-20261017``.
* CPU: ``5d0367f7ed06fbfe3f7ebabc28d6b110b2392eb2``, tag
  ``benchmarks/cpu-dense-scheduling-20261017``.

Later PR/rebase/merge heads identify different snapshots and are not substituted
for those compiled sources. Current-side framework comparisons below describe
these rejected B candidates; they are not final-production performance scores.

The initial CPU change was committed at 11:28 UTC, during baseline collection
(which began at 11:26 UTC). Its dense implementation and tests are byte-identical
to those in the later compiled CPU candidate. No baseline or component timing
outcome was inspected to select or revise that CPU change before its fixed A/B
plan. The plan's shorthand ``chosen before current-main baseline`` refers to
baseline outcome inspection; it does not place the draft commit before collection.

The :doc:`complete baseline <main-complete-20261017>` retains all 1,088 matched
framework pairs and all 135 higher native medians at the measured A source.
The source is more specific than a shared package version or the word main.

The :doc:`same-call CUDA diagnostics <main-cuda-phases-20261017>` retain all
16 captures/160 calls. Fresh cuBLAS includes handle creation and release;
the experiment preserves the full 4 MiB workspace and ``:4096:8`` environment.
The :doc:`CPU component diagnostics <main-cpu-components-20261017>` retain
78 matched pairs/156 independent-oracle observations and portable GNU16
code-generation evidence. Fewer loop backedges did not establish an accepted
whole-CG improvement. Component and instrumented phase medians are not added,
subtracted across scopes, or substituted for the completed-solve adoption gate.

Correctness and retained initial sanitizer failure
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

The measured CUDA candidate passed 129 CTest entries, including its full
Python suite (737 passed, 9 explicit skips), lint and public typing. GPU
memcheck and synccheck each covered 37 tests: 36 passed and the test requiring
two devices explicitly skipped on the single RTX2060. Both reported zero
errors; memcheck reported zero leaked bytes. This does not prove multi-GPU
callback restoration on hardware unavailable here.

Before A/B collection, the first test revision
``d6995cf0a0482d498a62fe0b3bbabd16e6ff8482`` deliberately called an invalid CUDA
device. Its retained memcheck run reported two API errors and zero leaked bytes.
Removing that redundant private negative test produced the measured ``3551afc``
revision; the production candidate was unchanged. Public invalid-context tests
remain, and sanitizer settings were not weakened or suppressed.
The original failure logs and subsequent zero-error logs remain local evidence:
``cuda-guards-memcheck.log``, ``cuda-guards-fixed-memcheck.log`` and
``cuda-guards-fixed-synccheck.log`` under ``build/main-performance-20261017/``.
This initial correctness failure was not an A/B timing trial or a discarded
performance result.

The CPU candidate passed all three CPU preset suites (93 CTest entries each)
and every pre/post long-double component oracle. These correctness results do
not waive either performance rejection. Final integration, relocated consumers,
distributions and documentation are checked separately on the retained source.

Fixed comparison protocol and complete evidence
-----------------------------------------------

Every campaign retains its complete preplanned A,B,B,A order, all native
candidates and framework controls, two fresh-process blocks per side and
every declared fixture. No failed comparison or regression is discarded.

Each block contains 36 complete Williams rounds, one solve per round, three
warmups and seed 20261017. Strict A and B summaries pool their 72 normalized
round samples. This estimator differs from the median of two process medians
used in the earlier descriptive baseline. The A samples here are new runs in
each ABBA campaign; historical baseline samples are not pooled into them.

The frozen production comparator uses the original 5% budget, 95% hierarchical
percentile intervals, 10,000 resamples and seed 20261017. These saved intervals
are audited without recalculation. Per-case intervals are not simultaneous
coverage, serial round dependence can narrow them, and two artifact blocks
give limited evidence of process variance. Control drift is not subtracted.

Known iterations/matvecs/restarts must match all four observations for each
same candidate/configuration. Different frameworks/scopes can perform different
accepted-tolerance work. No pure-kernel normalization or cross-scope strict
comparison bypasses a failed gate. Resident CUDA scopes keep transfers outside
timing; Session still creates fresh output and optional Jacobi while reusing
only iterative workspace. Device execution remains explicit and does not
change the Torch reference implementation.

CPU OpenMP uses two threads and logical affinity [0,2]; other presets use one
thread/[0]. Compiler, runtime, package and full threadpool controls are audited
per preset/suite/configuration. WSL affinity is not physical-host isolation.
Thermals, clocks, Windows display and host load remain uncontrolled. Campaigns
with different actual sources stay separate; no universal speedup is claimed.

cuda-abba (cuda) fixed A/B campaign
-----------------------------------

Whole-campaign verdict: **regression**.
All 128 artifacts/1344 candidate/case outcomes are retained.

Declared selection context: Initial DeviceGuard query-for-set candidate; redundant private invalid-device test removed before timing, with initial sanitizer failure retained; no performance outcome used to revise production code

Same-spec public reproduction
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

Use a fresh root checkout with the project-local CPU/CUDA environments and
toolkit described in :doc:`../install` and :doc:`../cuda`. The following
creates two detached sources; both use the same absolute local environments.
Build both sources and all selected presets before quiet timing. Never
point BUILD_DIR at a frozen evidence stage. Unique outputs and the complete
ABBA order below preserve all controls; new timings are not historical replay.

.. code-block:: sh

   PROJECT="$PWD"
   git fetch origin 445275a3994e342b06de4093889f3f814c8706c6 3551afcfee3faa6d4aa718cf9315782cc2ebb001
   A_SOURCE="$PROJECT/build/reproduce-cuda-abba-A"
   B_SOURCE="$PROJECT/build/reproduce-cuda-abba-B"
   git worktree add --detach "$A_SOURCE" 445275a3994e342b06de4093889f3f814c8706c6
   git worktree add --detach "$B_SOURCE" 3551afcfee3faa6d4aa718cf9315782cc2ebb001
   export OMP_DYNAMIC=FALSE MKL_DYNAMIC=FALSE
   export CUBLAS_WORKSPACE_CONFIG=:4096:8
   unset CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING

   (cd "$A_SOURCE" && env OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 MKL_NUM_THREADS=1 BLIS_NUM_THREADS=1 VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS=1 NUMEXPR_NUM_THREADS=1 taskset -c 0 make pyprof PRESET=cuda-release PYTHON="$PROJECT/.venv-cuda/bin/python" BUILD_DIR=build/reproduce-cuda-release PYPROF_SUITE=cuda-dense-f64-v1 PYPROF_SCOPE=api PYPROF_THREADS=1 PYPROF_ROUNDS=36 PYPROF_REPETITIONS=1 PYPROF_OUTPUT=build/ab-reproduction/cuda-release-cuda-dense-f64-v1-none-api-fused1-host-standard-b01-baseline.json 'PYPROF_ARGS=--implementations cuda-native,cuda-cublas,torch-cuda-eager --seed 20261017 --algorithm standard --fused --preconditioner none --rtol 1e-8 --atol 1e-12 --maxiter 2000 --residual-refresh 50 --warmups 3 --device cuda --cuda-device 0 --cuda-execution-mode host' CUDA_ROOT="$PROJECT/.deps/cuda-12.8" CUDA_HOST_COMPILER=/usr/bin/g++-13 CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=75 'CMAKE_ARGS=-DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=/usr/bin/g++-13 -DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS= '"'"'-DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS_RELEASE=-O3 -DNDEBUG'"'"' -DCMAKE_CUDA_FLAGS= '"'"'-DCMAKE_CUDA_FLAGS_RELEASE=-O3 -DNDEBUG'"'"'')
   (cd "$B_SOURCE" && env OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 MKL_NUM_THREADS=1 BLIS_NUM_THREADS=1 VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS=1 NUMEXPR_NUM_THREADS=1 taskset -c 0 make pyprof PRESET=cuda-release PYTHON="$PROJECT/.venv-cuda/bin/python" BUILD_DIR=build/reproduce-cuda-release PYPROF_SUITE=cuda-dense-f64-v1 PYPROF_SCOPE=api PYPROF_THREADS=1 PYPROF_ROUNDS=36 PYPROF_REPETITIONS=1 PYPROF_OUTPUT=build/ab-reproduction/cuda-release-cuda-dense-f64-v1-none-api-fused1-host-standard-b02-current.json 'PYPROF_ARGS=--implementations cuda-native,cuda-cublas,torch-cuda-eager --seed 20261017 --algorithm standard --fused --preconditioner none --rtol 1e-8 --atol 1e-12 --maxiter 2000 --residual-refresh 50 --warmups 3 --device cuda --cuda-device 0 --cuda-execution-mode host' CUDA_ROOT="$PROJECT/.deps/cuda-12.8" CUDA_HOST_COMPILER=/usr/bin/g++-13 CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=75 'CMAKE_ARGS=-DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=/usr/bin/g++-13 -DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS= '"'"'-DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS_RELEASE=-O3 -DNDEBUG'"'"' -DCMAKE_CUDA_FLAGS= '"'"'-DCMAKE_CUDA_FLAGS_RELEASE=-O3 -DNDEBUG'"'"'')
   (cd "$B_SOURCE" && env OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 MKL_NUM_THREADS=1 BLIS_NUM_THREADS=1 VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS=1 NUMEXPR_NUM_THREADS=1 taskset -c 0 make pyprof PRESET=cuda-release PYTHON="$PROJECT/.venv-cuda/bin/python" BUILD_DIR=build/reproduce-cuda-release PYPROF_SUITE=cuda-dense-f64-v1 PYPROF_SCOPE=api PYPROF_THREADS=1 PYPROF_ROUNDS=36 PYPROF_REPETITIONS=1 PYPROF_OUTPUT=build/ab-reproduction/cuda-release-cuda-dense-f64-v1-none-api-fused1-host-standard-b03-current.json 'PYPROF_ARGS=--implementations cuda-native,cuda-cublas,torch-cuda-eager --seed 20261017 --algorithm standard --fused --preconditioner none --rtol 1e-8 --atol 1e-12 --maxiter 2000 --residual-refresh 50 --warmups 3 --device cuda --cuda-device 0 --cuda-execution-mode host' CUDA_ROOT="$PROJECT/.deps/cuda-12.8" CUDA_HOST_COMPILER=/usr/bin/g++-13 CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=75 'CMAKE_ARGS=-DCMAKE_CXX_COMPILER=/usr/bin/g++-13 -DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS= '"'"'-DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS_RELEASE=-O3 -DNDEBUG'"'"' -DCMAKE_CUDA_FLAGS= '"'"'-DCMAKE_CUDA_FLAGS_RELEASE=-O3 -DNDEBUG'"'"'')

Compare each configuration's two complete A outputs against its two B outputs
with that B source's unchanged ``scripts/compare_performance.py`` and its
matching local Python/package. Use ``--max-regression .05``,
``--bootstrap-samples 10000`` and ``--seed 20261017``. All exact comparator
argv and original file paths remain in the analysis JSON.


cpu-abba (cpu) fixed A/B campaign
---------------------------------

Whole-campaign verdict: **regression**.
All 144 artifacts/1920 candidate/case outcomes are retained.

Declared selection context: Initial CPU two-group column scheduling candidate, preserving original two-row four-lane accumulation order; chosen before current-main baseline and before either component timing outcome was examined; whole fixed CPU inventory and controls retained

Same-spec public reproduction
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

Use a fresh root checkout with the project-local CPU/CUDA environments and
toolkit described in :doc:`../install` and :doc:`../cuda`. The following
creates two detached sources; both use the same absolute local environments.
Build both sources and all selected presets before quiet timing. Never
point BUILD_DIR at a frozen evidence stage. Unique outputs and the complete
ABBA order below preserve all controls; new timings are not historical replay.

.. code-block:: sh

   PROJECT="$PWD"
   git fetch origin 445275a3994e342b06de4093889f3f814c8706c6 5d0367f7ed06fbfe3f7ebabc28d6b110b2392eb2
   A_SOURCE="$PROJECT/build/reproduce-cpu-abba-A"
   B_SOURCE="$PROJECT/build/reproduce-cpu-abba-B"
   git worktree add --detach "$A_SOURCE" 445275a3994e342b06de4093889f3f814c8706c6
   git worktree add --detach "$B_SOURCE" 5d0367f7ed06fbfe3f7ebabc28d6b110b2392eb2
   export OMP_DYNAMIC=FALSE MKL_DYNAMIC=FALSE
   export CUBLAS_WORKSPACE_CONFIG=:4096:8
   unset CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING
